total prizemoney

contribution to horse and
participant welfare.

prizemoney, greatest first. In the case of horses
with the same number of career starts, age and
aggregate prizemoney, elimination shall be by lot.
The race will not be divided.

winner of any Listed race of 2000 metres or further
to carry 1kg extra. Penalties not to be cumulative.

Jump-Out Schedule
Division of Races Policy at Country TAB Race Meetings
• A race may be divided where the number of acceptors is 28 or greater.
• When assessing acceptance numbers, the day before rule will be taken into account (e.g. an acceptance total of 28 with two horses in the day before may not
be divided). For races on a Monday or Tuesday the number of horses accepted in the preceding Friday, Saturday and Sunday meetings will also be taken into
account when assessing acceptance numbers.
• As a general rule, if acceptance numbers are equal, preference for division will be given to two and three year old maidens.
• Future racing opportunities and recent lost opportunities in the region or surrounding regions will be taken into account when allocating a division.
• As a general rule, no more than nine races will be conducted on a Country TAB meeting. However 10 races may be considered if the starting time of the meeting
isn’t before 11am as a result of the division.
• A race may be divided outside of the above guidelines to make a seventh or eighth race at a TAB meeting or for reasons not specified above.
• As a general rule, no more than eight races will be conducted on a Country non-TAB meeting that is covered on Sky 2.
• RV’s budget constraints in relation to monthly divisions will also be taken into consideration, meaning that even if a race meets the above requirements for
division if the budgeted allocation for the month has been exhausted it may not be divided.

INDUSTRY NOTICE
Statistical analysis has identified that horses that have not had a race start by the time they turn five years old to be at approximately 4.4 times
the risk of catastrophic limb injury during racing compared to horses that have competed in a race prior to the time they turn five years old.
The calculated risk is higher again for unraced horses at six years old (7.2 times greater) and seven years old (11.9 times greater).
Because of the increased risk of catastrophic limb injury during racing for horses aged 5-years-old or older who have not previously completed
a race start, trainers and owners are advised that these horses are required to complete the following steps listed below. Steps 1-4 are to be
completed in order and within a reasonable time period that would reflect a routine single uninterrupted preparation:
1. 	Provide a satisfactory Veterinary Clearance prior to trialling (Jump Out or Official Trial). The trainer should inform Racing Victoria (RV) of
any relevant information (i.e. veterinary incidents including but not limited to respiratory surgery, tendon or ligament injury, stress fractures,
fractures, arthroscopy, etc) as to why the horse has not previously started in a race.
2. 	Horse to trial satisfactorily over the flat.
3. 	Provide a satisfactory Veterinary Clearance post-trial.
4. 	Provide a satisfactory report on a full body scintigraphy prior to racing.
5. 	Present for a pre-race veterinary inspection on course at first start.
Please note that further diagnostic tests may be indicated depending on the results of the scintigraphy.
EXEMPTIONS
On receipt and after consideration of an application for exemption, Racing Victoria may exempt a five-year-old horse from one or more of the
protocol steps with limitations. Horses aged six or older will not be exempted from any steps. The age of any horse is calculated from 1 August of
the current racing season.
Additionally, it is noted that:
1. 	Any horse that is unraced and has not completed the necessary steps in accordance with the protocol and has not been exempted from such
steps is ineligible to commence racing in Victoria until the steps are completed to the satisfaction of the Stewards.
2. 	Any horse that has not completed the necessary steps in accordance with the protocol and has not been exempted from such steps and has
raced in another jurisdiction may be required to complete any step, or any additional steps, as determined by RV Stewards to be eligible to
commence racing in Victoria. For example, a Veterinary Clearance from a Specialist Equine Veterinarian indicating that the horse is suitable
to continue to race may be required prior to racing in Victoria.
3. 	When a horse has been granted an exemption from any of the steps of the protocol, the Stewards may alter one or any of the remaining
steps to ensure the welfare of the horse. For example, a horse granted exemption from Step 4 (scintigraphy) may still be required to obtain a
Veterinary Clearance from a Specialist Equine Veterinarian at Step 3.
HORSES THAT HAVE NOT RACED FOR 12 MONTHS OR LONGER
Statistical analysis has also identified that when a horse has not raced for 12 months or longer it is also at an increased risk of catastrophic limb
injury during racing.
Because of these risks, the Australian Rules of Racing prescribe that for these horses a satisfactory Veterinary Clearance must be obtained, and
the horse must also trial satisfactorily prior to racing.
Following a satisfactory trial and upon receiving a satisfactory post-trial veterinary examination report, the Stewards may grant written
permission for the horse to be nominated for and participate in a race.
If a horse has been retired, trainers are reminded to advise RV and update the horse’s Stable Return accordingly, as is required by the Australian
Rules of Racing. If you have any questions, please contact veterinaryadmin@racingvictoria.net.au.
Unraced horses aged five years old and above
Notices | INTEGRITY SERVICES

===== PAGE 53 =====
From the Stewards’ Room
Horses not Having Started for over 12 Months
Veterinary clearance before trialing
The trainer of a horse that has not raced for 12 months or longer must under AR 88B produce to the Stewards a veterinary clearance as well as
any other information, examination or report they require, from a qualified veterinary surgeon indicating that the horse is suitable to resume
trialing and racing. Details of any injury or medical condition which might have caused the prolonged break from training or racing must be
provided with the veterinary clearance.
Following receipt of a satisfactory veterinary clearance, the Stewards may grant written permission for the horse to be nominated for and
participate in an official trial or jump out.
Please note, this veterinary clearance must be received prior to the horse performing in a jump out or trial.
Satisfactory trial
If permission to trial is granted by the Stewards, the horse must:
(a) participate in an official trial or jump out to the satisfaction of the Stewards; and
(b) have a post-trial veterinary examination (conducted by a qualified veterinary surgeon) to ensure that the horse is suitable to continue its
training regime.
Permission to race
Following a satisfactory trial and upon receiving the above satisfactory post-trial veterinary examination report, the Stewards may grant written
permission for the horse to be nominated for and participate in a race.
Horses Returning from Overseas
Trainers need to be aware of AR67 regarding horses returning from overseas. If they have a horse under their care which last started at an
international venue they must contact the RV Stewards or Racing Operations Departments to ensure that all previous form is updated on the
Racing Australia System.
Reporting of offensive emails
Racing Victoria Integrity Department are aware of licensed persons receiving abusive emails from punters which have been aggressive and
occasionally even threatening. Recipients of these emails are encouraged in the first instance to contact the Racing Victoria Intelligence Unit at
intelligenceunit@racingvictoria.net.au. The RV Intelligence Unit is keeping a log of the emails and also where possible try to identify the author
(which can be very difficult due to fake names and email addresses being used), some however have been identified and as a result action has
been taken against them.
In addition to the above, should an email contain threatening material or an intention to harm or damage, the recipient is strongly encouraged
to contact Victoria Police directly to report the matter. Racing Victoria are unable to submit a complaint on the recipients behalf as it must be
directly reported by them to Victoria Police.

AR 79 HORSES WITH BLEEDING
(1) The appearance of blood at both nostrils,
irrespective of quantity, is deemed to
constitute an attack of bleeding unless, in the
opinion of the Stewards, that bleeding was
caused by external trauma.
(2) If a horse has an attack of bleeding at any
time it must be reported by the trainer to the
Stewards without delay.
(3) If a PRA advises in writing that a horse has
had an attack of bleeding, that advice will be
prima facie evidence that that horse has had
an attack of bleeding.
(4) A horse which, in the opinion of the
Stewards, has had an attack of bleeding;
(a) for a period of 2 months after the
attack of bleeding, must not, without the
permission of the Stewards, be trained,
exercised or galloped on a racecourse,
recognised training track, private training
establishment, or other place;
(b) must not start in any race for a period
of 3 months, and then only after:
(i) a gallop of at least 1,000 metres; or
(ii) an official trial or jump-out:
in the presence, and to the satisfaction
of a Steward.
(5) Following a horse having an attack of
bleeding the Stewards must:
(a) retain a written record of the attack
of bleeding and any related restriction
imposed on the horse (and if applicable,
also record that attack of bleeding and
any restriction imposed in the horse’s
Thoroughbred Identification Card, which
must be provided by the trainer to the
Stewards as soon as possible after the
attack of bleeding); and
(b) record any subsequent permission
given for the horse to resume racing (and if
applicable, also record that on the horse’s
Thoroughbred Identification Card, which
must be provided by the trainer to the
Stewards as soon as possible).
(6) If a horse has more than one attack of
bleeding, the horse is ineligible to start in any
race.
(7) If a horse displays blood at one nostril,
the trainer must report that to the Stewards
without delay.
(8) Unless the Stewards are satisfied that the
presence of blood referred to in subrule (7) is
attributable to external trauma, then before
racing again the horse is required to
(a) Undergo a gallop of at least 1,000
metres; or
(b) participate in an official trial or jump-
out; in the presence, and to the satisfaction
of a Steward.
EMBARGOES AND VETERINARY
CERTIFICATES
Trainers are reminded to ensure that any
embargo or veterinary certificate which has
been placed on a runner in their care has been
lifted or submitted prior to accepting for any
future race. If you have any queries, please
contact the RV Stewards Department on 03
92584258.
USE OF PHONE IN MOUNTING YARD
PROHIBITED
Trainers and their representatives are
reminded that they must restrict recording of
riders’ post-race comments to areas outside
horse confines of mounting yard when horses
return after race.
STABLE EMPLOYEE REGISTRATIONS
Trainers, please be aware, that it is their
responsibility to ensure all their stable
staff have up to date Stable Employee
registrations for the new season. Stewards
will be conducting regular checks to ensure all
registrations are up to date.
LR 74A PROCEDURE FOR HORSES
CARRYING OVERWEIGHT IN FLAT
RACES
Trainers and riders should now be aware
of the following rule amendment which
came into effect from 1 June 2025
regarding the procedure for horses carrying
overweight in flat races. Previously, the
process for managing rider overweights
between acceptance time and race day
lacked consistency across meetings and
administrative clarity for participants
and officials. This resulted in confusion,
late changes, and perceived inequity.
Consultation was undertaken with both the
Victorian Jockeys Association (VJA) and
the Australian Trainers’ Association (ATA)
and both stakeholder groups are supportive
of the below rule amendments and its
intended implementation. Additionally, a
Board endorsed trial period was successfully
conducted which demonstrated the
effectiveness in streamlining processes and
ensuring fairness. Following this trial period,
feedback from stakeholders was positive and
accordingly, The Racing Victoria Board has
approved the following Local Rule of Racing
amendments effective from 1 June 2025.
LR 74A PROCEDURE FOR HORSES
CARRYING OVERWEIGHT IN FLAT
RACES
(1) Subject to the approval of the nominator
or trainer of a horse engaged to race at a
Victorian race meeting, a rider, excluding
apprentice jockeys, may:
a. declare up to 1kg overweight from
the handicapped weight released
at acceptances, provided that such
declaration must be made by no later
than 2:00pm on the day of acceptances.
(2) If an overweight declaration is made,
that declared weight will be published as the
probable weight, with the weight confirmed
at the time of weighing out for such race.
(3) In accordance with LR 42, an apprentice
jockey is not permitted to make an
overweight declaration.
(4) A claiming apprentice jockey may only
replace the overweight rider on race day
if no other rider is available to ride at the
handicapped weight.
(5) No overweight approvals will be
granted for any rider declared to ride at a
horse’s true weight (Saturday Metropolitan
Meetings only).
(6) Subject to LR 74A (1)(a), the Stewards
will not permit a rider to ride overweight
except with the permission of the Stewards
which may be granted only in special
circumstances.
